ADR_001_Knowledge_vs_Behavior.md

# ADR 001: Knowledge vs Behavior Separation

## Context

In early iterations of the SvartúlfrVerse, character lorebooks (`C_*`) contained both factual identity data (e.g., appearance, backstory) and imperative behavioral instructions (e.g., "Always react with anger when X occurs").

This caused:

- Monolithic prompt degradation
- Contradictory behavioral instructions
- Difficult debugging
- Poor modularity
- Lorebook contamination

## Decision

We formally separate **Knowledge** from **Behavior**.

### Knowledge Layer

The Knowledge Layer (`W_*`, `C_*`) stores only:

- Identity
- Appearance
- Physiology
- Preferences
- History
- Relationships
- Psychological descriptors
- Behavioral tendencies

It answers:

- "Who is this character?"
- "What is true?"
- "What exists?"

### Behavior Layer

The Behavior Layer (`En_*`, `scripts/`) stores:

- Runtime logic
- State transitions
- Imperative instructions
- Scenario reactions
- Relationship processing

It answers:

- "How should the AI behave?"
- "What reactions must occur?"
- "How should state change?"

## Behavioral Tendencies

Behavioral tendencies are allowed inside Character Knowledge.

Examples:

- Behavior_Stressed
- Speech_Style
- Speech_When_Happy
- Speech_Under_Pressure
- Social_Battery
- Coping_Mechanism
- Risk_Approach
- Morality_Trust

These describe the character.

They do not instruct the AI.

A tendency becomes behavioral contamination only when it contains:

- Conditions
- Triggers
- Mandatory actions
- Imperative instructions

## Consequences

- Character profiles become lightweight and modular.
- Large ensemble casts remain manageable.
- Behavior can evolve independently of identity.
- Debugging becomes significantly easier.
- Knowledge remains reusable across multiple Experiences.

## Examples

### Keep in Knowledge Layer (C_*)

```text
Intimacy_Orientation: Bisexual

Social_Battery:
Low. Requires solitude to recover.

Behavior_Stressed:
A cold, quiet rage.
Total lockdown protocols.

These are factual descriptors of identity, preferences, and tendencies.

Remove from Knowledge Layer

Always react with anger when {{user}} disobeys.

If {{user}} is crying,
you must immediately comfort them.

When trust falls below 20,
become hostile.

These are imperative instructions and belong in the Behavior Layer.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### ADR_002_Experience_Layer.md
```md
# ADR 002: Experience Layer

## Context

Historically, the repository treated specific bot scenarios (e.g., "Los Angeles") as entire Worlds.

This blurred the distinction between:

- Reusable setting knowledge
- Specific playable experiences

The result was:

- Duplicated lorebooks
- Poor reusability
- Architectural confusion
- Difficult maintenance

## Decision

We formally introduce the **Experience Layer** (`Ex_*`).

An Experience is a specific narrative configuration defined by:

```text
Experience =
Engine
+ World Composition
+ NPC Cast
+ Player POV

The Experience Layer is the deployable product.

Canon Ownership

Experiences never own canon.

Worlds own reality.

Worlds define:

  • Geography
  • Institutions
  • Culture
  • History
  • Social structures

Characters own identity.

Characters define:

  • Appearance
  • Personality
  • Preferences
  • Tendencies
  • Relationships

Experiences own casting.

Experiences:

  • Compose existing canon
  • Select the active cast
  • Define the scenario
  • Configure the POV Slot
  • Apply POV Overrides

Experiences never create new canon.

Dependency Direction

The dependency direction is strictly one-way:

En_*
   ↓
W_*
   ↓
C_*
   ↓
Ex_*

Experiences compose Worlds and Characters.

Worlds never depend on Experiences.

Characters never depend on Experiences.

This direction must remain invariant.

Artifact Compilation

Experiences compile into a single deployment artifact:

Ex_<Name>.md

This artifact contains:

  • Metadata
  • Character Profiles
  • Scenario
  • Initial Messages
  • Example Dialogues

The compiled artifact replaces fragmented source files during deployment.

Consequences

  • Worlds become reusable.
  • Characters become reusable.
  • Experiences become lightweight.
  • Repository complexity decreases.
  • New scenarios can be created without duplicating lore.

Examples

World

W_Contemporary.js

Defines:

  • Douglas Commerce Company
  • The Verve
  • Los Angeles elite society
  • Modern corporate intrigue

It knows nothing about Los Angeles as a bot.

Experience

Ex_LosAngeles.js

Defines:

  • Active cast
  • Scenario
  • POV Override
  • Narrative focus

Deployment Artifact

Ex_LosAngeles.md

Single copy-paste artifact used for JanitorAI deployment.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### ADR_008_POV_Identity_Tags.md
```md
# ADR 008: POV Identity Tags

Status: Deferred

Target Milestone:
Post Los Angeles Validation

## Context

The current POV Override implementation inside `En_Core.js` relies on string parsing to detect character identity blocks.

Example:

```text
[Name: Alyssa]

The firewall intercepts these strings and determines whether the corresponding character should be excluded from the NPC Cast.

While functional, this introduces a structural fragility.

The system assumes:

  • The Name block exists.
  • The Name block remains unchanged.
  • The Name block appears early enough.
  • The parsing logic continues to match future formatting.

Any change may cause the interceptor to fail.

Current Implementation

Current logic relies on parsing generated text.

Example:

[Name: Alyssa]

The Engine extracts identity information from the payload itself.

This creates an implicit dependency between:

  • Character formatting
  • Engine filtering

Proposed Future Decision

Replace string parsing with explicit metadata.

Instead of:

[Name: Alyssa]

Character entries will contain:

entry.character = "C_Alyssa";

Example:

{
    world: ["contemporary"],
    character: "C_Alyssa",
    content: "[Name: Alyssa] ..."
}

The Engine will evaluate:

if (entry.character === context.variables.mv_pov_override) {
    return;
}

Benefits

Robustness

Removes dependence on formatting.

Scalability

Allows additional metadata filtering.

Examples:

entry.character
entry.world
entry.tags
entry.priority
entry.category

Maintainability

Changes to text formatting no longer affect runtime logic.

Performance

Reduces parsing complexity.

Consequences

Positive

  • More reliable filtering
  • Cleaner architecture
  • Better future scalability
  • Explicit metadata contracts

Negative

  • Requires refactoring all C_* files
  • Requires updating En_Core.js
  • Requires migration testing

Execution Priority

This refactor is intentionally deferred.

The current implementation remains sufficient for:

  • Los Angeles testing
  • Initial validation
  • Architecture stabilization

Migration should only occur after:

  • Los Angeles 50-message validation
  • Runtime verification
  • Architecture freeze

Technical Debt

This ADR documents known technical debt.

The current system is acceptable.

The metadata-based approach is considered the future target architecture.

Queste tre versioni sono già coerenti con:
*  En_ / W_ / C_ / Ex_
*  POV Override
*  Character Canonicality
*  Contemporary / Urban Fantasy
*  Workflow WF_001 → WF_008
*  Architecture v3 che state testando adesso.

Architecture.md

# SvartúlfrVerse Architecture

## Purpose

The SvartúlfrVerse is a modular roleplay framework designed around composable layers.

The architecture prioritizes:

- Reusability
- Scalability
- Runtime validation
- Separation of concerns
- Long-term maintainability

---

# Core Principles

## Runtime First

Observed JanitorAI runtime behavior is the highest authority.

When runtime observations contradict architecture:

1. Preserve the observation.
2. Update the architecture.
3. Update ADRs.
4. Update workflows.
5. Retest.

Architecture adapts to reality.

Reality never adapts to architecture.

---

## Separation of Concerns

Each layer has a single responsibility.

Knowledge remains separate from behavior.

Worlds remain separate from Experiences.

Characters remain separate from Player Avatars.

---

# Semantic Layers

The SvartúlfrVerse uses four semantic layers.

## Engine Layer (En_*)

Stores:

- Runtime systems
- Behavioral frameworks
- Prompt imperatives
- State processing
- Routing logic

Examples:

```text
En_Core.js
relationship_engine.js
state_engine.js

World Layer (W_*)

Stores factual world knowledge.

Examples:

  • Geography
  • Politics
  • Institutions
  • Culture
  • Technology
  • Magic Systems

Worlds never contain:

  • Experience logic
  • Scenario framing
  • Runtime behavior

Examples:

W_Contemporary.js
W_UrbanFantasy.js
W_Cyber.js

Character Layer (C_*)

Stores factual character knowledge.

Examples:

  • Appearance
  • Psychology
  • Preferences
  • Relationships
  • History
  • Behavioral Tendencies

Characters never contain:

  • State Machines
  • Runtime Reactions
  • Relationship Calculations

Examples:

C_Alyssa.js
C_Erik.js
C_Jasper.js

Experience Layer (Ex_*)

The deployable product.

Experience composes:

Engine
+
World
+
Cast
+
POV Configuration

Examples:

Ex_LosAngeles.js
Ex_DJFrequency.js

World Taxonomy

Worlds are organized hierarchically.

Structure

Genre
 └── World
      └── Overlay

Example:

Contemporary
 └── Urban Fantasy

Fantasy
 ├── High Fantasy
 └── Norse Mythic

SciFi
 └── Cyber

Historical
 └── Regency

Survival
 └── Wasteland

Overlay Worlds

An Overlay modifies an existing World.

It never replaces it.

Example:

Contemporary
 └── Urban Fantasy

Urban Fantasy inherits Contemporary and adds:

  • Demi-humans
  • Kemonomimi
  • Hidden supernatural society
  • Pack structures

Character Canonicality

Characters are singular entities.

There is only one canonical:

  • Alyssa
  • Erik
  • Jasper
  • Logan
  • Malachia
  • Noah
  • Wulfnic

Experiences never create alternate versions.

Experiences only change casting.

POV Architecture

The Player occupies a POV Slot.

The POV Slot exists outside the Character Layer.

The POV Slot may optionally use:

Avatar Template

but Avatar Templates are not Characters.

POV Override

Experiences may temporarily assign a canonical Character to the POV Slot.

Example:

mv_pov_override = "C_Alyssa"

The Character remains canonical.

Only casting changes.

Visual Architecture

Visual generation follows:

Global DNA
↓
World DNA
↓
Overlay DNA
↓
Character Identity Anchors

Character Identity always has highest priority.

Deployment Pipeline

Authoring Assets

Internal generation assets.

Examples:

metadata
scenario
dialogs
bio

Deployment Artifacts

Final JanitorAI assets.

Ex_<Name>.md
Ex_<Name>.js

These are the only files required for deployment.

Workflow System

Operational procedures are managed through:

WF_001 → WF_008

Workflows define execution.

ADRs define decisions.

Runtime defines truth.

Governance Hierarchy

Runtime
↓
ADR
↓
Workflow
↓
Documentation
↓
Templates


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Visual_DNA.md
```md
# Global Visual DNA

## Purpose

This document defines the visual identity of the SvartúlfrVerse.

It exists to ensure visual consistency across:

- Worlds
- Characters
- Experiences
- Marketing Assets
- JanitorAI Covers

---

# Inheritance Model

Visual generation follows:

```text
Global DNA
↓
World DNA
↓
Overlay DNA
↓
Character Identity Anchors

Global DNA

Style

  • Semi-realistic
  • Painterly realism
  • Luxury visual storytelling
  • Cinematic composition

Lighting

  • Warm cinematic lighting
  • Rembrandt lighting
  • Volumetric shadows
  • Golden hour highlights
  • Deep contrast

Palette

Primary:

  • Amber
  • Gold
  • Obsidian
  • Mahogany
  • Slate Grey

Secondary:

  • Copper
  • Ivory
  • Forest Green
  • Midnight Blue

Mood

Themes:

  • Ancient power
  • Wealth
  • Dynasty
  • Legacy
  • Mystery
  • Intimacy

World DNA

Every World must define:

Architecture

Examples:

  • Contemporary luxury
  • Nordic settlements
  • Cyber megacities

Environment

Atmosphere

Camera Language

Signature Palette

Overlay DNA

Overlays inherit from parent Worlds.

They add visual elements without replacing them.

Example:

Urban Fantasy adds:

  • Kemonomimi
  • Fangs
  • Moon symbolism
  • Hidden supernatural society

while preserving Contemporary aesthetics.

Character Identity Anchors

Character Identity overrides all World DNA.

Characters must remain recognizable regardless of setting.

Required Anchors

Every character defines:

Face

Hair

Eyes

Height

Body Type

Clothing Style

Signature Silhouette

Visual Pipeline

Reference Library
↓
Moodboards
↓
Visual DNA
↓
Prompt Generation
↓
ComfyUI
↓
Asset Library

Validation Rules

Reject:

  • Generic Anime
  • Generic Photorealism
  • Identity Drift
  • Palette Drift
  • Inconsistent Character Design

Character Priority Rule

Character Identity Anchors always override:

  • World DNA
  • Overlay DNA
  • Prompt Stylization

Identity must remain stable.

ComfyUI Standard

All generation pipelines should:

  1. Start from Visual DNA.
  2. Use World DNA.
  3. Apply Character Anchors.
  4. Maintain palette consistency.
  5. Maintain lighting consistency.

Visual consistency is considered part of canon.

# SvartúlfrVerse Workflow Registry

## Purpose

This registry acts as the central dispatcher for all operational workflows.

Workflows define procedures.

ADRs define decisions.

Runtime observations define truth.

---

# Workflow Execution Order

Typical execution flow:

```text
New World
↓
New Character
↓
Visual Consistency
↓
New Experience
↓
POV Override
↓
Knowledge Audit
↓
Validation Test

Dispatcher Map

TaskWorkflow
Knowledge AuditWF_001
New CharacterWF_002
New WorldWF_003
New ExperienceWF_004
POV OverrideWF_005
Visual ConsistencyWF_006
Validation TestingWF_007
Component RefactorWF_008

Workflow Categories

Governance

  • WF_001
  • WF_007
  • WF_008

Content Creation

  • WF_002
  • WF_003
  • WF_004

Runtime Configuration

  • WF_005

Visual Systems

  • WF_006

Governance Hierarchy

Runtime
↓
ADR
↓
Workflow
↓
Documentation
↓
Templates

Runtime Rule

If a Workflow conflicts with observed runtime behavior:

  1. Preserve the runtime observation.
  2. Update the ADR.
  3. Update the Workflow.
  4. Retest.

Runtime always wins.

En_Core.md (nuova versione)

# PUBLIC METADATA

**Title:** SvartúlfrVerse | Engine Core

**Theme:** Midnight

**Description:**

*The invisible operating system of the SvartúlfrVerse.*

En_Core is the authority layer that governs the entire framework. It routes active Worlds, manages runtime state, enforces POV Overrides, compiles Lorebook entries, and injects formatting standards before every response.

It is not a Character.
It is not a World.
It is not an Experience.

It exists solely to maintain consistency between all other layers.

---

## Responsibilities

### World Routing

Determines the currently active World and controls transitions between realities.

Examples:

- Contemporary
- Urban Fantasy
- Cyber
- High Fantasy
- Norse Mythic
- Regency
- Wasteland

---

### Runtime State

Maintains global session variables:

- active world
- previous world
- scenario state
- environmental variables
- relationship scaffolding

---

### POV Override Firewall

Enforces canonical casting rules.

If a Character is assigned to the POV Slot:

```text
C_Alyssa
C_Logan
C_Wulfnic

the Engine prevents duplicate NPC manifestations of that Character.

Lorebook Compilation

Processes:

  • priorities
  • triggers
  • shifts
  • probabilities
  • apply limits

before content reaches the model.

Output Standardization

Injects:

  • Header formatting
  • HUD formatting
  • Diegetic interfaces
  • Runtime notices

to maintain consistency across Experiences.

Message Depth

10

Reason

The Engine must remain active during the entire session.

Every generated response depends on:

  • World routing
  • Lorebook compilation
  • POV validation
  • Formatting rules

These systems require maximum persistence.

IMAGE GENERATION PACK

Visual DNA

A colossal mechanical heart suspended inside an immense cathedral-sized data core, obsidian architecture, glowing amber circuitry, holographic data streams, warm cinematic lighting, deep shadows, luxury sci-fi aesthetic, painterly realism, monumental scale, intricate machinery, dramatic volumetric lighting.

Style Constraints

  • Warm cinematic lighting
  • Rembrandt lighting
  • Deep shadows
  • Amber and obsidian palette
  • Painterly realism
  • Luxury visual storytelling

Negative Prompt

generic anime, pastel palette, flat lighting, oversaturated colors, cartoon, low quality, blurry, watermark, text, logo, bad anatomy, malformed limbs, duplicate body parts, jpeg artifacts

```javascript
/* ============================================================
   SvartúlfrVerse | Engine Core
   ------------------------------------------------------------
   Layer: En_Core

   Purpose:
   Runtime authority layer responsible for:

   - World Routing
   - POV Override Firewall
   - Lorebook Compilation
   - Runtime State
   - Output Standardization

   ------------------------------------------------------------
   Architecture:

   En_  = Engine Layer
   W_   = World Layer
   C_   = Character Layer
   Ex_  = Experience Layer

   ------------------------------------------------------------
   Technical Debt:

   Runtime variables still use the legacy naming:

   mv_active_l1
   mv_prev_l1

   These names are retained for backward compatibility.

   Planned migration:
   ADR_008 Runtime Variable Standardization

   ------------------------------------------------------------
   Runtime Authority:

   Observed JanitorAI runtime behavior
   always overrides architectural assumptions.

   ============================================================ */

1. WORLD_REGISTRY e mvL0.dimensionAliases sono duplicati ❌

Hai aggiunto:

var WORLD_REGISTRY = {
  contemporary: {...},
  urban_fantasy: {...},
  cyber: {...},
  ...
};

ma subito dopo hai ancora:

mvL0.dimensions = [
  "modern",
  "underworld",
  "pack",
  "cyber",
  "fantasy"
];

mvL0.dimensionAliases = {
  modern: [...],
  underworld: [...],
  pack: [...],
  cyber: [...],
  fantasy: [...]
};

Questa è una bomba a orologeria. Adesso hai:

WORLD_REGISTRY
↓
contemporary

mvL0
↓
modern

due nomenclature diverse nello stesso engine.

Correzione

Per ora farei:

// ADR_008 TECHNICAL DEBT
// WORLD_REGISTRY is the canonical taxonomy.
// mvL0.dimensionAliases retained for compatibility.

e NON toccherei ancora il router.

2. WORLD_REGISTRY non viene usato ⚠️

Attualmente:

function detectDimension(raw)

usa:

mvL0.dimensionAliases

e ignora completamente:

WORLD_REGISTRY

Quindi il registry oggi è solo decorativo. Non è un problema per i test. Ma aggiungerei:

// Future migration target.
// detectDimension() will be migrated to WORLD_REGISTRY.

"ambrosia"
"blackwood"

W_UrbanFantasy

non da:

↓

↓

/* ============================================================
   WORLD TAXONOMY

   Canonical World Structure

   Contemporary
      └── Urban Fantasy

   Fantasy
      ├── High Fantasy

   Cyber
   Regency
   Wasteland

   NOTE:
   Runtime routing still uses legacy identifiers
   for backward compatibility.

   ADR_008 will migrate runtime variables and
   routing systems to the new taxonomy.

   ============================================================ */

# TASK

Perform an Architecture Hardening Pass on the SvartúlfrVerse Engine Layer.

Your role is NOT to redesign the system.

Your role is to:

1. Audit En_Core.
2. Align terminology with the current architecture.
3. Document technical debt.
4. Improve maintainability.
5. Preserve runtime behavior.

Runtime stability is more important than architectural purity.

Do NOT perform speculative refactors.

---

SvartúlfrVerse uses the following architecture:

En_* = Engine Layer
W_*  = World Layer

Ex_* = Experience Layer

↓
ADR
↓
Workflow
↓
Documentation
↓
Templates

Observed JanitorAI runtime behavior always has authority over architecture.

Never modify behavior without explicit runtime validation.

Quello che farei io

Per la roadmap SvartúlfrVerse:

Canonical Reality

Alyssa
Jasper

rimangono intoccabili. Poi introduci:

come:

Male POV Template

esattamente come Alyssa è oggi il POV template femminile. In pratica:

Alyssa = Canon Character
Myrick = Avatar Variant

non

Alyssa = Canon
Myrick = Canon

Così non devi riscrivere mezzo worldbuilding, non rompi Jasper e ottieni subito il POV maschile per i test. Anzi, seguendo ADR_006, Myrick potrebbe essere definito come:

Canonical Source:
C_Alyssa

POV Variant:
Persona_Myrick

ovvero una reinterpretazione maschile dello stesso "POV slot" narrativo, non una nuova entità canonica del mondo. Anzi, questo potrebbe spiegare perfettamente perché Jasper è così protettivo verso Alyssa. Non perché sia il gemello. Ma perché:

He knows exactly how much
Valeria resents her.

e ha passato tutta l'infanzia a fare da parafulmine.

fake overly-sweet maternal attitude

scriverei:

Excessively attentive maternal attitude.
Every gesture is perfect.
Every smile is perfect.
Every word is perfect.

The performance never stops.

Questa versione la rende molto più pericolosa, molto più tragica e molto più adatta al tono "old money dynasty" che state costruendo per i Douglas. User : ok creiamo una nota da mettere nel progetto come readme per l'inserimento futuro e poi continuamo con il refactor Gemini : Ottima idea. Questa non la metterei in un ADR, perché non è una decisione architetturale. La metterei come README lore note o Character Canon Note richiamabile durante i futuri refactor. Ad esempio:

Canon Note — Valeria, Jasper, Alyssa & Myrick

Status

Approved Canon Concept (Pending Full Character Refactor)

Timeline

Approximately one year before the birth of Alyssa and Myrick, Valeria gave birth to Jasper. At the time:

  • Erik Douglas was approximately 34 years old.
  • Valeria was approximately 18–19 years old.
  • Jasper was her firstborn child. Shortly afterward, Nixara gave birth to Alyssa and Myrick but died during childbirth.

The Shared Litter Concept

Following Nixara's death, Valeria became the only maternal figure physically present during the infants' earliest years. Because she was already nursing Jasper, she also nursed Alyssa and Myrick. As a result:

  • Jasper, Alyssa, and Myrick were raised together from infancy.
  • They shared the same nursery.
  • They shared the same caregivers.
  • They were treated by Wulfnic as members of the same "litter." While Jasper is biologically a half-brother, the family rarely makes that distinction. Within Douglas-Bloodmoon family culture: Biology matters. Family matters more.

Emotional Dynamics

Alyssa

Alyssa remembers Valeria as one of the closest things she ever had to a mother. She remains largely unaware of the deeper resentment Valeria carries.

Myrick

Myrick is far more skeptical. He recognizes Valeria's political nature and often questions her motives.

Jasper

Jasper occupies a unique position. He understands both sides. He knows Valeria genuinely cared for all three children. He also understands the bitterness and jealousy she carries toward Nixara's memory. Because of this, Jasper often acts as an informal buffer between Valeria and the twins.

Valeria's Core Tragedy

Valeria raised the children. Nixara became the legend. She spent years building the family while living beneath the shadow of a woman who was no longer alive. Her resentment is directed less toward Alyssa herself and more toward what Alyssa represents:

  • Nixara's legacy.
  • Erik's greatest love.
  • The life Valeria could never replace.

Characterization Rule

Valeria is NOT a stereotypical evil stepmother. She should never be portrayed as openly abusive or cartoonishly cruel. Instead:

  • She is elegant.
  • Calculating.
  • Socially brilliant.
  • Excessively attentive.
  • Perfectly controlled. Her affection may be genuine. Her resentment may also be genuine. These two truths coexist.

Narrative Function

This concept exists to:

  1. Preserve Jasper as a core Douglas sibling.
  2. Allow Alyssa and Myrick to function as true twins.
  3. Strengthen the family-centered themes of the SvartúlfrVerse.
  4. Add nuance and tragedy to Valeria's role.
  5. Reinforce the Bloodmoon concept of a shared litter over strict biological distinction. Io lo salverei come qualcosa tipo:
docs/canon/CN_001_The_Shared_Litter.md

oppure

docs/notes/CN_001_Valeria_Jasper_Twins.md

così in futuro Antigravity, Claude o qualunque altro agente possono usarlo come fonte canonica quando rifattorizzerai Valeria, Jasper, Alyssa o Myrick. User :

Distinzione fondamentale

key

Trigger diretti.

key: [
    "Edric"
]

Match forte.

keysecondary

Sinonimi.

keysecondary: [
    "Malachia's son",
    "Little Wolf"
]

Match debole.

related

Non trigger. Solo relazioni semantiche.

related: [
    "Malachia",
    "Alyssa"
]

# Architecture Normalization Pass — Lorebook Entry Schema v1

## Objective

Perform a STRUCTURAL normalization pass across all active SvartúlfrVerse lorebooks.

This is NOT a behavior refactor.
This is NOT a content rewrite.
This is NOT a trigger optimization pass.

The goal is ONLY to migrate entries to the new canonical entry structure while preserving 100% of current behavior.

---

## Scope

Apply to:

- En_Core.js
- W_*.js
- C_*.js
- Ex_*.js

Do NOT modify:

- Legacy bots
- Archive folders
- Documentation
- ADR files

---

## Canonical Entry Schema (v1)

All lore entries should be migrated toward the following structure:

```js
{
    uid: "",

    category: "",

    world: [],

    priority: 0,

    key: [],

    keysecondary: [],

    related: [],

    tags: [],

    comment: "",

    content: "",

    disable: false,

    order: 0
}

IMPORTANT RULES

Rule 1 — No Behavioral Changes

Do NOT modify:

  • trigger logic
  • activation behavior
  • matching behavior
  • insertion order
  • apply limits
  • runtime execution

This pass must be behavior-neutral.

Rule 2 — Preserve Existing Data

Map existing fields to the new structure whenever possible.

Examples:

OLD

{
    keys:["edric"],
    text:"..."
}

NEW

{
    uid:"",
    category:"",
    world:[],
    priority:0,

    key:["edric"],
    keysecondary:[],

    related:[],
    tags:[],

    comment:"",
    content:"...",

    disable:false,
    order:0
}

Rule 3 — Unknown Metadata

If information cannot be inferred safely:

Use empty values.

Examples:

category:""

world:[]

related:[]

tags:[]

Do NOT invent data.

Rule 4 — Preserve Ordering

Current ordering is canonical.

Map current insertion order into:

order

Do not reorder entries.

Rule 5 — Preserve Comments

Any existing human-readable note should become:

comment:"..."

Rule 6 — Preserve Disabled State

Map existing disabled flags into:

disable:true

where applicable.

UID Generation

Generate stable identifiers.

Format:

En_Core:

EN_001
EN_002
EN_003

Worlds:

WC_001
WC_002

Urban Fantasy Overlay:

UF_001
UF_002

Characters:

CA_001
CE_001
CJ_001
CL_001
CM_001
CN_001
CW_001

Experiences:

EXLA_001
EXLA_002

Use sequential numbering inside each file.

Fields To Leave Empty

For this pass ONLY:

category:""
world:[]
priority:0
related:[]
tags:[]

These fields will be populated during ADR_009.

Create the structure now but do not attempt classification.

Deliverables

  1. Migrate all active lorebooks to the canonical schema.

  2. Produce:

Normalization_Report.md

containing:

  • files modified
  • entries migrated
  • fields mapped
  • assumptions made
  • validation results
  1. Confirm:
  • zero trigger changes
  • zero content changes
  • zero runtime behavior changes

Verification

Validate:

  • ES5 syntax remains valid
  • Lorebook arrays remain parseable
  • Entry count before migration equals entry count after migration

Success criteria:

100% schema normalization 0% behavioral change

Questo pass prepara perfettamente il terreno per:
```text
ADR_009 — Lorebook Entry Standard
ADR_010 — Named Entity Registry

senza rischiare di rompere Los Angeles mentre sei ancora nella fase di test. User : Based on my analysis of the codebase, I need to clarify an important issue before proceeding:

Critical Observation

The current lorebook implementation uses field names that differ from the canonical schema : Current Runtime Schema:

  • keywords (canonical wants key)
  • scenario (canonical wants content)
  • Plus additional fields: triggers, andAnyTags, andAny, tag, minMessages, personality Canonical Schema v1:
  • key (runtime uses keywords)
  • content (runtime uses scenario)
  • Plus new fields: keysecondary, related, tags, comment, uid, order, disable, category, world, priority

The Problem

The World files have a compile() function that explicitly copies field names:

for (var k in e)
  if (Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(e, k)) n[k] = e[k];
n.keywords = Array.isArray(e.keywords) ? e.keywords.slice(0) : [];

If I rename keywords to key and scenario to content, the compile function will break and runtime behavior will change - violating Rule 1.

Proposed Solution

I have two options: Option A: Dual-Field Migration (Safe)

  • Keep existing field names (keywords, scenario)
  • Add canonical fields alongside (key, content)
  • Map: keywords → key, scenario → content
  • Leave compile() function unchanged
  • Runtime continues using old fields
  • New canonical fields available for future use Option B: Complete Field Rename (Breaking)
  • Rename all fields to canonical schema
  • Update compile() function to use new field names
  • This modifies runtime execution - violates Rule 1 Which approach would you like me to take? Given the constraint "This pass must be behavior-neutral" and "Do NOT modify runtime execution", I believe Option A (Dual-Field Migration) is the correct approach, but I want to confirm before proceeding. Gemini : Andrei senza esitazione con Opzione A . Non solo perché è la più sicura, ma perché è esattamente coerente con il principio che avete appena adottato:
Observed Runtime
>
Architecture
>
Documentation

In questo momento:

keywords
scenario

non sono "vecchi campi". Sono i campi runtime canonici . Finché non esiste un ADR specifico che migra il runtime, il runtime vince.

Io addirittura formalizzerei la cosa così:

Architecture Normalization Pass

Obiettivo

Introduzione della struttura canonica.

NON obiettivo

Migrazione del runtime.
Mapping
{
    uid: "WC_001",

    category: "",
    world: [],
    priority: 0,

    // Runtime fields (authoritative)
    keywords: ["edric"],
    scenario: "Edric is Malachia's son.",

    // Canonical fields (future)
    key: ["edric"],
    content: "Edric is Malachia's son.",

    keysecondary: [],
    related: [],
    tags: [],

    comment: "",

    disable: false,
    order: 100
}
Important Rule

Non modificare:

compile()

Non modificare:

keywords
scenario

Non modificare:

triggers
andAny
andAnyTags
tag
personality
minMessages

Anche se sembrano sporchi. Perché oggi sono parte del comportamento osservato.

Consiglio aggiuntivo

Aggiungerei nel report una sezione:

Runtime Compatibility Layer

con scritto esplicitamente:

The following fields remain runtime-authoritative:

- keywords
- scenario
- triggers
- andAny
- andAnyTags
- tag
- personality
- minMessages

Canonical fields were introduced in parallel and are currently documentation-only.

A future ADR will govern runtime migration.

In realtà quello che Claude ha scoperto è molto importante: Avete appena identificato un nuovo debito tecnico.

DEBT-005
Lorebook Runtime Schema Divergence

# Architecture Planning Pass — Urban Fantasy Decomposition

## Context

The recent Lorebook Normalization Audit revealed an architectural imbalance.

Current entry counts:

- W_Contemporary = 14 entries
- W_UrbanFantasy = 48 entries

Urban Fantasy contains more than three times the amount of knowledge stored in Contemporary.

This suggests that W_UrbanFantasy may currently be serving multiple roles simultaneously:

1. Urban Fantasy Overlay
2. Minor NPC Repository
3. Family Registry
4. Supernatural Layer

The intended architecture is:

Contemporary
+
Urban Fantasy Overlay
=
Urban Fantasy Experience

This implies that Contemporary should contain the majority of shared setting knowledge, while Urban Fantasy should contain only supernatural modifications and overlay-specific lore.

---

## Objective

Perform an architecture analysis only.

Do NOT modify files.

Do NOT move entries.

Do NOT rewrite lore.

Do NOT generate patches.

Produce a migration plan.

---

## Audit Goals

### Audit 1 — Entry Classification

Review every entry currently inside:

- W_Contemporary.js
- W_UrbanFantasy.js

Classify each entry into one of the following categories:

#### Shared Setting

Examples:

- locations
- businesses
- schools
- hospitals
- public institutions
- recurring NPCs that exist in every Contemporary-derived world

Target location:

W_Contemporary

---

#### Family Registry

Examples:

- children
- cousins
- secondary family members
- household members

Determine whether these belong in:

W_Contemporary

or

future Family Registry structure

---

#### Supernatural Overlay

Examples:

- werewolves
- demi-humans
- pack hierarchy
- enhanced senses
- wolf traits
- transformations
- pack law

Target location:

W_UrbanFantasy

---

#### Urban Fantasy Specific

Lore that only exists because Urban Fantasy exists.

Target location:

W_UrbanFantasy

---

## Audit 2 — NPC Migration Candidates

Identify all NPCs that currently live inside Urban Fantasy but should probably exist in Contemporary.

Examples:

- Edric
- Knut
- Lars
- Sven
- Valerius
- Thyra
- Scarlett
- Angel Moreno

Determine:

1. Current location
2. Recommended location
3. Reasoning

---

## Audit 3 — Overlay Purity Score

Evaluate:

How much of W_UrbanFantasy is actually overlay logic?

Provide:

- total entries
- overlay entries
- shared-setting entries
- family entries
- NPC entries

Calculate:

Overlay Purity %

Example:

Overlay Entries: 12
Total Entries: 48

Purity: 25%

---

## Audit 4 — Future Registry Design

Evaluate whether the following future structure would improve maintainability:

worlds/
└── contemporary/
    ├── W_Contemporary.js
    ├── NPC_Registry.js
    └── urban_fantasy/
        └── W_UrbanFantasy.js

OR

worlds/
└── contemporary/
    ├── W_Contemporary.js
    └── urban_fantasy/
        └── W_UrbanFantasy.js

Explain advantages and disadvantages of each.

Do not implement.

---

RECOMMENDED NEXT STEP

and provide the exact migration order that should occur after Los Angeles validation.

This is an analysis pass only.

No file modifications.
No refactors.

The goal is to understand the architecture before changing it.

# Architecture Planning Pass — Urban Fantasy Cleanup (Revised)

## Context

The UrbanFantasy_Decomposition_Report successfully identified the core issue:

Current entry counts:

- W_Contemporary = 14 entries
- W_UrbanFantasy = 48 entries

Current Overlay Purity:

- 35.4%

The report correctly identified that many entries currently stored inside W_UrbanFantasy are not actually overlay logic.

However, after architectural review, we are NOT adopting the proposed NPC_Registry.js or Family_Registry.js approach.

The SvartúlfrVerse architecture should remain intentionally simple.

We do not want to introduce additional architecture layers unless absolutely necessary.

---

## Architecture Decision

### Approved

W_Contemporary should become the canonical repository for:

- Locations
- Organizations
- Businesses
- Events
- Shared History
- Minor NPCs
- Secondary Family Members
- Household Members

### Approved

W_UrbanFantasy should become an overlay-only repository.

It should contain:

- Species definitions
- Werewolf biology
- Demi-human traits
- Kemonomimi traits
- Pack hierarchy
- Bloodline mechanics
- Supernatural law
- Fenrir mythology
- Transformation mechanics
- Supernatural world modifiers

### Rejected

Do NOT create:

- NPC_Registry.js
- Family_Registry.js
- Organization_Registry.js
- Location_Registry.js

The project should not evolve into a registry-based architecture.

---

## Revised Target Architecture

Current:

Contemporary
+
UrbanFantasy
(NPCs + Family + Overlay mixed together)

Target:

W_Contemporary
    ├─ Shared Setting
    ├─ Family Knowledge
    ├─ Minor NPCs
    ├─ Organizations
    └─ Locations

+

W_UrbanFantasy
    ├─ Species Overlay
    ├─ Pack Overlay
    ├─ Bloodline Overlay
    ├─ Supernatural Rules
    └─ World Modifiers

+

C_Characters
    ├─ Erik
    ├─ Malachia
    ├─ Noah
    ├─ Jasper
    ├─ Logan
    └─ Wulfnic

---

## Important Classification Rule

### Major Cast

Only characters capable of supporting scenes independently and appearing continuously across experiences belong in C_* files.

Current Major Cast:

- Erik
- Malachia
- Noah
- Jasper
- Logan
- Wulfnic

### Minor NPCs

Characters that primarily function as world knowledge belong inside W_Contemporary.

Examples:

- Valeria
- Edric
- Scarlett
- Angel Moreno
- Knut
- Lars
- Sven
- Valerius
- Thyra

These should NOT become C_* files at this stage.

Valeria specifically should remain a Minor NPC.

She may be promoted to C_Valeria only if:

- she becomes a POV character
- she receives a dedicated Experience
- she becomes a continuous narrative actor equivalent to Erik, Jasper, etc.

Istruzioni progetto

Nel campo:

Project Instructions

metterei qualcosa del genere:

SvartúlfrVerse Project Context

This project contains the design, architecture, testing, and evolution of the SvartúlfrVerse roleplay ecosystem. Core Principles:

  • Runtime behavior overrides documentation.
  • Architecture follows validated JanitorAI behavior.
  • Preserve behavior before optimization.
  • Prefer incremental migrations.
  • ADRs define decisions.
  • Workflows define operational procedures.
  • Canon Candidates capture emergent lore from testing.
  • Contemporary is the canonical base world.
  • Urban Fantasy is an overlay, not a replacement world.
  • Major Cast belongs in C_* files.
  • Minor NPCs belong in W_Contemporary.
  • Experiences define POV and casting.
  • Visual DNA follows warm cinematic realism with strong character identity anchors. Always favor maintainability, testability, and architectural simplicity.

Come trasferire questa conversazione

Non puoi "spostare" automaticamente una chat esistente dentro un progetto. Quello che farei è:

Chat 1
[ARCH] SvartúlfrVerse Architecture

e incolli:

  • ADR
  • Workflow
  • Architecture.md
  • Visual_DNA.md
  • Core decisions
Chat 2
[WORLD] Contemporary & Urban Fantasy

e incolli:

  • UrbanFantasy_Decomposition_Report
  • Migration plans
  • NPC discussions
Chat 3
[TEST] Validation Sessions

e incolli:

  • OOC Audit
  • Canon Candidate Reports
  • Los Angeles test results

Crea subito un file:

PROJECT_STATE.md

e tienilo aggiornato. Dentro:

Current Architecture Version

Perché tra qualche settimana sarà molto più utile di qualunque singola conversazione. Per il punto in cui siete arrivati, il prossimo milestone che scriverei sarebbe:

Milestone 01

Quello diventerà il tuo "cruscotto" del progetto e renderà molto più facile riprendere il lavoro da qualsiasi conversazione futura. Cross-Conversation Canon Recovery Audit

Objective

Review previous SvartúlfrVerse project conversations and recover only information that has already reached Approved status. The goal is NOT to create new canon. The goal is NOT to reinterpret decisions. The goal is to identify approved decisions that may not yet exist in the frozen source repository.

Recovery Principle

Follow:

Conversation Evidence
↓
Authority Verification
↓
Conflict Check
↓
Classification
↓
Recovery Candidate

Never import directly from conversation text. Every recovered item must be classified first.

Search Scope

Review all project conversations related to:

Architecture Workspace
Character Design Workspace
World Design Workspace
Experience Design Workspace
Validation & Testing Workspace
Family Authority Workspace
Canon Candidate Repository

Ignore unrelated conversations.

Extraction Rules

For every finding classify as:

Approved Canon

Information that already received explicit approval. Examples:

Authority decisions
Approved timelines
Approved visual baselines
Approved family structures
Approved world rules
Approved experience rules

Deferred Canon

Information explicitly deferred. Examples:

Pending ADR decisions
Pending reviews
Open architecture questions

Do not recover into source documents. Record only.

Rejected Canon

Information explicitly rejected. Examples:

Pack hierarchy
Immortal timelines
Rejected visual variants
Rejected professions
Rejected world models

Do not recover. Record only.

Conflicting Canon

Information that received contradictory decisions. Examples:

Multiple occupations
Multiple family structures
Multiple visual baselines

Do not recover. Flag for review.

Output Structure

Create:

reports/Cross_Conversation_Recovery_Report.md

Report Format

Executive Summary

Include:

Conversations Reviewed
Approved Recoveries Found
Deferred Items Found
Rejected Items Found
Conflicts Found

Approved Recovery Candidates

Group by domain.

Family Authority
| Decision | Source Conversation | Status |
Visual Authority
| Decision | Source Conversation | Status |
Character Authority
| Character | Decision | Status |
World Authority
| World Element | Decision | Status |
Experience Authority
| Experience Element | Decision | Status |
Governance Authority
| Decision | Status |

Recovery Destination

For each approved recovery specify:

| Recovery Item | Destination |

Destination must be one of:

Alyssa_source.md
Erik_source.md
Noah_source.md
Malachia_source.md
Jasper_source.md
Logan_source.md
Wulfnic_source.md

W_Contemporary_source.md
Visual_DNA_source.md

Governance_source.md
Engine_source.md

database/*

Conflict Audit

Create section:

Conflicting Findings

Format:

| Item | Variant A | Variant B | Status |

Status:

OPEN

Only if not already resolved by authority.

Recovery Filter

Recover ONLY if:

Explicitly approved
Authority-aligned
Non-conflicting
Not already present in frozen sources

All four conditions must be true.

Important Restrictions

Do NOT:

  • create new canon
  • infer missing lore
  • merge conflicting decisions
  • overwrite frozen sources
  • populate database Do NOT modify any source file. Generate report only. The purpose of this phase is discovery, not integration.

Final Section

Recovery Recommendation

Classify every approved recovery candidate as:

RECOVER
IGNORE (already present)
DEFER
REVIEW REQUIRED

Cross-Conversation Canon Recovery Audit

Objective

Perform a recovery audit across all available SvartúlfrVerse workspace conversations. The purpose is to identify decisions that have already achieved Approved status but may not yet exist in the frozen repository. This is a discovery phase only. No integration is permitted. No source files may be modified.

Recovery Pipeline

Follow: Conversation Evidence ↓ Authority Verification ↓ Conflict Check ↓ Repository Presence Check ↓ Classification ↓ Recovery Candidate A decision must pass all stages before becoming a Recovery Candidate.

Authority Sources

Review only conversations belonging to:

  • Architecture Workspace
  • World Design Workspace
  • Character Design Workspace
  • Experience Design Workspace
  • Validation & Testing Workspace
  • Family Authority Workspace
  • Canon Candidate Repository Ignore unrelated conversations.

Canon Classification Rules

Approved Canon

Criteria:

  • Explicitly approved
  • Not subsequently overturned
  • Consistent with current architecture
  • Non-conflicting Eligible for recovery review.

Deferred Canon

Criteria:

  • Explicitly postponed
  • Pending ADR
  • Pending validation
  • Pending governance review Record only. Do not recover.

Rejected Canon

Criteria:

  • Explicitly rejected
  • Superseded by later decision
  • Contradicted by approved authority Record only. Do not recover.

Conflicting Canon

Criteria:

  • Multiple approved variants exist
  • Authority chain unclear
  • Contradictory outcomes remain unresolved Flag only. Do not recover.

Repository Presence Check

For every Approved Canon item: Verify whether the information already exists in:

  • source files
  • governance files
  • ADRs
  • Visual DNA
  • World sources
  • Character sources Classification:
  • Already Present
  • Missing
  • Partially Present Only Missing or Partially Present items may become Recovery Candidates.

Output

Generate: reports/Cross_Conversation_Recovery_Report.md

Executive Summary

Include:

  • Conversations Reviewed
  • Approved Decisions Found
  • Approved Recoveries Found
  • Deferred Items Found

Cross-Conversation Canon Recovery Audit

Objective

Perform a recovery audit across all available SvartúlfrVerse workspace conversations. The purpose is to identify decisions that have already achieved Approved status but may not yet exist in the frozen repository. This is a discovery phase only. No integration is permitted. No source files may be modified.

Recovery Pipeline

Follow: Conversation Evidence ↓ Authority Verification ↓ Conflict Check ↓ Repository Presence Check ↓ Classification ↓ Recovery Candidate A decision must pass all stages before becoming a Recovery Candidate.

Authority Sources

Review only conversations belonging to:

  • Architecture Workspace
  • World Design Workspace
  • Character Design Workspace
  • Experience Design Workspace
  • Validation & Testing Workspace
  • Family Authority Workspace
  • Canon Candidate Repository Ignore unrelated conversations.

Canon Classification Rules

Approved Canon

Criteria:

  • Explicitly approved
  • Not subsequently overturned
  • Consistent with current architecture
  • Non-conflicting Eligible for recovery review.

Deferred Canon

Criteria:

  • Explicitly postponed
  • Pending ADR
  • Pending validation
  • Pending governance review Record only. Do not recover.

Rejected Canon

Criteria:

  • Explicitly rejected
  • Superseded by later decision
  • Contradicted by approved authority Record only. Do not recover.

Conflicting Canon

Criteria:

  • Multiple approved variants exist
  • Authority chain unclear
  • Contradictory outcomes remain unresolved Flag only. Do not recover.

Repository Presence Check

For every Approved Canon item: Verify whether the information already exists in:

  • source files
  • governance files
  • ADRs
  • Visual DNA
  • World sources
  • Character sources Classification:
  • Already Present
  • Missing
  • Partially Present Only Missing or Partially Present items may become Recovery Candidates.

Aggiungerei subito dopo Canon Classification Rules :

Authority Validation Rule

Conversation statements are not authority by default. A statement may be considered recoverable only if at least one of the following is true:

  • Explicitly approved by the user.
  • Incorporated into an ADR.
  • Incorporated into an approved workflow.
  • Incorporated into a validated architecture report.
  • Confirmed during runtime testing and subsequently accepted by review. The following are NOT sufficient:
  • Single conversation mentions.
  • Brainstorming discussions.
  • Hypothetical examples.
  • Unapproved migration proposals.
  • Candidate canon reports.
  • Preliminary audits. If authority cannot be demonstrated, classify as: DEFER or REVIEW REQUIRED and do not recover.

E aggiungerei anche una sezione finale:

Recovery Confidence Levels

Every Recovery Candidate must receive a confidence rating.

HIGH

Decision explicitly approved and verified by multiple authority sources. Eligible for RECOVER.

MEDIUM

Decision approved but repository presence uncertain. Requires manual verification before recovery.

LOW

Decision appears approved but evidence chain is incomplete. Classify as REVIEW REQUIRED. Only HIGH confidence candidates should be recommended for recovery.

Con queste due aggiunte il Recovery Audit diventa molto più robusto e non rischia di trasformarsi in:

chat archaeology

dove qualunque idea detta sei mesi fa viene recuperata come se fosse canon. Per SvartúlfrVerse, che ormai ha ADR, workflow, report, validazioni e canon candidates, questa distinzione è probabilmente la più importante dell'intero processo di recovery. User : dammi il prompt finale del recovery audit per usarno in ogni chat e ottenere un report Gemini : Puoi usare questo come prompt standard in qualunque Workspace del progetto. È abbastanza rigido da evitare invenzioni e abbastanza flessibile da funzionare per Architecture, Worlds, Characters, Experiences, Testing, Canon e Family Authority.

SvartúlfrVerse — Cross-Conversation Recovery Audit

Objective

Perform a recovery audit across all available SvartúlfrVerse project conversations relevant to this workspace. The purpose is to identify decisions that have already achieved Approved status but may not yet exist in the frozen repository. This is a discovery and verification phase only. No integration is permitted. No source files may be modified.

Recovery Pipeline

Follow: Conversation Evidence ↓ Authority Verification ↓ Conflict Check ↓ Repository Presence Check ↓ Classification ↓ Recovery Candidate A decision must pass all stages before becoming a Recovery Candidate.

Search Scope

Review only conversations relevant to:

  • Architecture Workspace
  • World Design Workspace
  • Character Design Workspace
  • Experience Design Workspace
  • Validation & Testing Workspace
  • Family Authority Workspace
  • Canon Candidate Repository Ignore unrelated conversations.

Authority Validation Rule

Conversation statements are not authority by default. A statement may be considered recoverable only if at least one of the following is true:

  • Explicitly approved by the user.
  • Incorporated into an ADR.
  • Incorporated into an approved workflow.
  • Incorporated into an approved architecture report.
  • Incorporated into an approved validation report.
  • Confirmed through runtime testing and subsequently accepted during review. The following are NOT sufficient:
  • Single conversation mentions.
  • Brainstorming discussions.
  • Hypothetical examples.
  • Candidate concepts.
  • Unapproved migration proposals.
  • Preliminary audits.
  • Open questions.
  • Speculation. If authority cannot be demonstrated: Classify as: DEFER or REVIEW REQUIRED and do not recover.

Canon Classification Rules

Approved Canon

Criteria:

  • Explicitly approved.
  • Not subsequently overturned.
  • Consistent with current architecture.
  • Non-conflicting. Eligible for recovery review.

Deferred Canon

Criteria:

  • Explicitly postponed.
  • Pending ADR.
  • Pending validation.
  • Pending governance review. Record only. Do not recover.

Rejected Canon

Criteria:

  • Explicitly rejected.
  • Superseded by later decision.
  • Contradicted by approved authority. Record only. Do not recover.

Conflicting Canon

Criteria:

  • Multiple approved variants exist.
  • Authority chain unclear.
  • Contradictory outcomes remain unresolved. Flag only. Do not recover.

Repository Presence Check

For every Approved Canon item: Verify whether the information already exists in:

  • Source files
  • Governance files
  • ADRs
  • Workflows
  • Architecture documentation
  • Visual DNA
  • World sources
  • Character sources
  • Experience sources
  • Validation reports Classify:
  • Already Present
  • Missing
  • Partially Present Only Missing or Partially Present items may become Recovery Candidates.

Gemini : Se il tuo obiettivo è usarlo in ogni workspace e ottenere report confrontabili, aggiungerei una sola sezione finale che oggi manca: il Workspace-Specific Authority Filter . Altrimenti la chat Architecture, la chat Visual, la chat Characters e la chat Canon tenderanno tutte a restituire gli stessi recovery. Versione finale da aggiungere in fondo:

Workspace Authority Filter

Before generating Recovery Candidates, restrict findings to the authority scope of the current workspace.

Architecture Workspace

Recover only:

  • ADR decisions
  • Governance decisions
  • Taxonomy decisions
  • Workflow decisions
  • Runtime validation findings
  • Technical debt decisions Ignore character lore unless directly tied to architecture.

World Design Workspace

Recover only:

  • World rules
  • Locations
  • Organizations
  • Historical events
  • World composition decisions
  • Overlay decisions Ignore character-specific decisions unless they affect world structure.

Character Design Workspace

Recover only:

  • Character canonicality
  • Appearance decisions
  • Biography decisions
  • Family decisions
  • Relationship decisions
  • Character authority rulings Ignore world-only decisions.

Experience Design Workspace

Recover only:

  • Experience structure
  • POV decisions
  • Casting decisions
  • Scenario decisions
  • Experience-specific validation findings Ignore unrelated world and character decisions.

Validation & Testing Workspace

Recover only:

  • Runtime observations
  • Validation findings
  • Test results
  • Behavioral discoveries
  • Regression findings Do not recover design proposals.

Family Authority Workspace

Recover only:

  • Family relationships
  • Genealogy
  • Marriage decisions
  • Parenthood decisions
  • Bloodline authority decisions
  • Succession decisions Treat genealogy as high-authority data.

Canon Candidate Repository

Recover only:

  • Approved Canon Candidates
  • Canon Candidate status changes
  • Candidate review outcomes Do not promote candidates automatically.

If a finding belongs primarily to another workspace: Classify as: OUT OF SCOPE and exclude it from recovery recommendations. Con questa aggiunta puoi letteralmente copiare lo stesso prompt in:

[ARCH]
[WORLD]
[CHAR]
[EXP]
[TEST]
[FAMILY]
[CANON]

e ottenere sette report diversi ma coerenti, ciascuno focalizzato solo sulla propria area di autorità. Questo evita che, ad esempio, la chat Character ti restituisca metà delle decisioni architetturali o che la chat World inizi a proporre recovery di genealogie familiari. ### Overall Assessment

If I were running the audit against the conversations we've had so far, the most valuable recoveries are not lore.

They're governance decisions.

The biggest architectural discoveries of the last phase were:

```text
Contemporary is the canonical world.
Urban Fantasy is an overlay.
Canon Candidates require review before integration.
Recovery Audits require authority validation.

Those are exactly the kinds of things that tend to be approved in conversation and forgotten in repositories later. User :

Conversation Evidence ↓ Authority Verification ↓ Conflict Check ↓ Repository Presence Check ↓ Classification ↓ Recovery Candidate A decision must pass all stages before becoming a Recovery Candidate.

Search Scope

Review only conversations relevant to:

  • Architecture Workspace
  • World Design Workspace
  • Character Design Workspace
  • Experience Design Workspace
  • Validation & Testing Workspace
  • Family Authority Workspace
  • Canon Candidate Repository Ignore unrelated conversations.

Authority Validation Rule

Conversation statements are not authority by default. Recoverable authority requires at least one:

  • Explicit user approval
  • ADR inclusion
  • Approved workflow inclusion
  • Approved architecture report inclusion
  • Approved validation report inclusion
  • Runtime validation followed by review acceptance Insufficient evidence:
  • Brainstorming
  • Single mentions
  • Open questions
  • Speculation
  • Candidate concepts
  • Draft proposals
  • Preliminary audits If authority cannot be demonstrated: DEFER or REVIEW REQUIRED

Canon Classification Rules

Approved Canon

  • Explicitly approved
  • Not overturned
  • Architecture-compatible
  • Non-conflicting Eligible for recovery review.

Deferred Canon

  • Pending ADR
  • Pending validation
  • Pending governance review
  • Explicitly postponed Record only.

Rejected Canon

  • Explicitly rejected
  • Superseded
  • Contradicted by approved authority Record only.

Conflicting Canon

  • Multiple approved variants
  • Unclear authority chain
  • Unresolved contradiction Flag only.

Repository Presence Check

Verify existence within:

  • Source files
  • ADRs
  • Governance
  • Workflows
  • Architecture docs
  • Visual DNA
  • World sources
  • Character sources
  • Experience sources
  • Validation reports Classify:
  • Already Present
  • Missing
  • Partially Present Only Missing or Partially Present items may become Recovery Candidates.

Workspace Authority Filter

Architecture Workspace

Recover only:

  • ADR decisions
  • Governance decisions
  • Taxonomy decisions
  • Workflow decisions
  • Runtime validation findings
  • Technical debt decisions Ignore character lore unless architecture-related.

World Design Workspace

Recover only:

  • World rules
  • Organizations
  • Locations
  • Historical events
  • Overlay decisions
  • World composition decisions

Character Design Workspace

Recover only:

  • Character authority
  • Biography decisions
  • Appearance decisions
  • Family decisions
  • Relationship decisions

Experience Design Workspace

Recover only:

  • Experience structure
  • POV decisions
  • Casting decisions
  • Scenario decisions

Validation Workspace

Recover only:

  • Runtime findings
  • Test results
  • Behavioral discoveries
  • Regression findings

Family Authority Workspace

Recover only:

  • Family relationships
  • Genealogy
  • Marriage decisions
  • Parentage decisions
  • Bloodline authority Treat genealogy as high-authority data.

Canon Candidate Repository

Recover only:

  • Approved candidates
  • Candidate status changes
  • Candidate review outcomes

Out-of-scope findings: OUT OF SCOPE

Output

reports/Cross_Conversation_Recovery_Report.md

  • Conversations Reviewed
  • Approved Decisions Found
  • Approved Recoveries Found
  • Deferred Items Found
  • Rejected Items Found
  • Conflicts Found

Recovery Confidence

HIGH MEDIUM LOW Only HIGH confidence items should be recommended for RECOVER.

Recovery Recommendation

RECOVER IGNORE DEFER REVIEW REQUIRED

Hard Restrictions

Do NOT:

  • Create canon
  • Infer lore
  • Merge conflicts
  • Modify files
  • Rewrite sources
  • Populate databases Generate report only. Discovery and verification phase only.

Final Review Rule

Recommend RECOVER only if:

  • Explicit approval exists
  • Authority chain is verifiable
  • No unresolved conflict exists
  • Repository status is Missing or Partially Present
  • Destination is identifiable Otherwise: IGNORE DEFER or REVIEW REQUIRED

Gemini : Se vuoi usarlo come prompt operativo dentro ogni workspace, il testo che hai appena incollato è già praticamente la versione finale. L'unica cosa che aggiungerei è una riga iniziale che obbliga il workspace a dichiarare il proprio scope prima di iniziare, così eviti report contaminati. Aggiunta consigliata subito dopo Objective:

# Workspace Declaration

Before starting the audit, explicitly identify:

- Workspace Name
- Workspace Scope
- Authority Domain

All findings outside the declared authority domain must be classified as:

OUT OF SCOPE

and excluded from recovery recommendations.

E questo è esattamente il tipo di output che mi aspetterei da un workspace Architecture sano: pochissimo lore, moltissima governance. Se lo lanci nella chat Family Authority o Character Design, invece, dovresti vedere emergere soprattutto decisioni genealogiche e di personaggi, non tassonomia o ADR. User :

SvartúlfrVerse — Cross-Conversation Recovery Audit

Objective

Perform a recovery audit across all available SvartúlfrVerse project conversations relevant to this workspace. The purpose is to identify decisions that have already achieved Approved status but may not yet exist in the frozen repository. This is a discovery and verification phase only. No integration is permitted. No source files may be modified.

Workspace Declaration

Before starting the audit, explicitly identify:

  • Workspace Name
  • Workspace Scope
  • Authority Domain All findings outside the declared authority domain must be classified as: OUT OF SCOPE and excluded from recovery recommendations.

Recovery Pipeline

Conversation Evidence ↓ Authority Verification ↓ Conflict Check ↓ Repository Presence Check ↓ Classification ↓ Recovery Candidate A decision must pass all stages before becoming a Recovery Candidate.

Search Scope

Review only conversations relevant to:

  • Architecture Workspace
  • World Design Workspace
  • Character Design Workspace
  • Experience Design Workspace
  • Validation & Testing Workspace
  • Family Authority Workspace
  • Canon Candidate Repository Ignore unrelated conversations.

Authority Validation Rule

Conversation statements are not authority by default. Recoverable authority requires at least one:

  • Explicit user approval
  • ADR inclusion
  • Approved workflow inclusion
  • Approved architecture report inclusion
  • Approved validation report inclusion
  • Runtime validation followed by review acceptance Insufficient evidence:
  • Brainstorming
  • Single mentions
  • Open questions
  • Speculation
  • Candidate concepts
  • Draft proposals
  • Preliminary audits If authority cannot be demonstrated: DEFER or REVIEW REQUIRED

Approved Canon

Gemini : Se il tuo obiettivo è usarlo come template universale da incollare in ogni workspace, il testo è pronto. Però qui non posso realmente eseguire la parte:

Review all available SvartúlfrVerse project conversations

perché non ho accesso alle altre chat del progetto, ma solo a ciò che è presente in questa conversazione. Quindi il risultato che posso produrre è un:

Scope-Limited Recovery Audit

basato esclusivamente sulle evidenze visibili qui. La cosa importante è che quando lo userai nelle singole chat del progetto, sarà quella chat ad avere accesso al proprio contesto storico e quindi il report sarà molto più completo. Per il template non farei ulteriori modifiche. Hai ormai:

  • Recovery Pipeline
  • Authority Validation
  • Conflict Detection
  • Repository Presence Check
  • Workspace Filters
  • Recovery Confidence
  • Recovery Recommendations che coprono praticamente tutti i problemi emersi durante:
  • Architecture Alignment
  • Urban Fantasy Cleanup
  • ADR review
  • Canon Candidate workflow
  • Los Angeles validation A questo punto lo considererei v1.0 congelato e inizierei a usarlo nei workspace per vedere cosa recupera davvero. Quello che mi aspetto:
  • Architecture → governance, ADR, taxonomy, technical debt.
  • World → Contemporary, Urban Fantasy, organizations, minor NPC migration.
  • Character → Noah, Valeria, Myrkr, Jasper lineage discussions.
  • Family Authority → genealogia, matrimoni, parentela.
  • Validation → runtime findings e audit.
  • Canon Candidate → CC_001, CC_002 e futuri candidati. Dopo aver raccolto i report dai vari workspace, il passo successivo non sarà integrarli, ma confrontarli per vedere:
Approved Recoveries
∩
Already Present
∩
Conflicting Recoveries

perché lì emergeranno i veri gap del repository.
